Implement:
```
import numpy as np
import time

def _constant_candidate(n=1):
    return np.ones(max(1,int(n)), dtype=np.float64)

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 1.0)
    best = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    best_score = float(evaluate_sequence(best.tolist()))
    # try parent length constant if available
    try:
       p = globals().get('height_sequence_1')
       if p is not None:
           parr = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64)
           if parr.ndim >0:
              parr = parr.ravel()
              if parr.size >0 and np.isfinite(parr).all():
                  n = parr.size
                  if n <= 1000000: # memory safe list
                     cand = np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64)
                     # maybe clip? ones fine
                     s = float(evaluate_sequence(cand.tolist()))
                     if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
                        best, best_score = cand, s
    except Exception:
       pass
    # optional: if best_score > 2 due rounding, try tiny ones? n=1 exact
    if not np.isfinite(best_score) or best_score > 2.0:
       cand = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
       s = float(evaluate_sequence(cand.tolist()))
       if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
          best, best_score = cand, s
    # maybe try a small local search to see if any computed score <2? Cheap bounded loop?

Let's design final code robust and reasonably efficient:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _as_1d_finite(arr):
   try:
      a = np.asarray(arr, dtype=np.float64)
      if a.ndim != 1: a = a.ravel()
      if a.size == 0: return None
      if not np.isfinite(a).all(): return None
      return a
   except Exception:
      return None

def _eval_candidate(seq):
   try:
      # seq is list of float
      return float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
   except Exception:
      return float('inf')

def _candidate_list(a):
   return [float(x) for x in a]

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
   deadline = time.monotonic() + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 2.0)
   best = None
   best_score = float('inf')

   def consider(a):
      nonlocal best, best_score
      if a is None: return
      if time.monotonic() > deadline: return
      a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
      if a.ndim !=1: a = a.ravel()
      if a.size ==0 or not np.isfinite(a).all(): return
      a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
      if float(np.sum(a)) < 0.01: return
      s = _eval_candidate([float(x) for x in a])
      if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
          best = a.copy()
          best_score = s
          return
```
But calling consider on parent large maybe expensive. Fine. However, `consider` uses `nonlocal` in nested function; Python 3 okay.

import time

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    candidate = [1.0]

        s = evaluate_sequence(candidate)
        if np.isfinite(float(s)):
            return candidate
    except Exception:
        pass

    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()

        parent = parent[np.isfinite(parent)]
        if parent.size > 0 and float(parent.sum()) >= 0.01:
            return [float(x) for x in parent]

        pass

Let's write:
```
import numpy as np
import time

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    # The exact objective is >=2 for every nonnegative finite sequence:
    # max conv >= sum a^2 and (sum a)^2 <= n sum a^2.
    # Therefore any positive constant sequence is globally optimal. Use the
    # smallest valid one so that both proposal and evaluation remain O(1).
    best = [1.0]
    try:
        score = float(evaluate_sequence(best))
        if np.isfinite(score):
            return best
    except Exception:
        pass

    # If the evaluator is unavailable for some unexpected reason, still return
    # a valid finite nonnegative list.
    return best
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 1.0)
    unit = [1.0]
    best = unit
    best_score = float('inf')
    try:
        s = evaluate_sequence(unit)
        s = float(s)
        if np.isfinite(s):
            best_score = s
    except Exception:
        pass
    try:
        if time.monotonic() < deadline:
            parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
            if parent is not None:
                p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64)
                if p.ndim != 1:
                    p = p.ravel()
                # Only spend O(n^2) on moderate-sized parents.
                if 0 < p.size <= 10000 and np.isfinite(p).all():
                    pc = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
                    if float(np.sum(pc)) >= 0.01:
                        try:
                            ps = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in pc]))
                            if np.isfinite(ps) and ps < best_score:
                                best = pc
                                best_score = ps
                        except Exception:
                            pass
    except Exception:
        pass
    # Final safe verification of chosen best; if it fails, fall back to unit.
    try:
        if best is None:
            return unit
        arr = np.asarray(best, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(arr).all():
            return unit
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if float(np.sum(arr)) < 0.01:
            return unit
        # If best has many entries, final evaluation could be expensive. We only
        # evaluate modest best; unit is safe. We know best is unit or <=10000.
        if arr.size <= 10000:
            s = float(evaluate_sequence([float(x) for x in arr]))
            if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_score:
                best_score = s
        else:
            # if somehow large, skip convolve; unit verified already.
            pass
    except Exception:
        return unit
    # Return Python floats
    try:
        out = [float(x) for x in np.asarray(best, dtype=np.float64).ravel()]
        if out and all(np.isfinite(x) for x in out):
            return out
    except Exception:
        pass
    return unit
```

Let's craft final code cleanly:

```
import numpy as np
import time

def _eval_list(seq):
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 2.0)

    unit = [1.0]
    best = unit
    best_score = _eval_list(unit)

    parent = None
    try:
        parent = globals().get("height_sequence_1")
    except Exception:
        parent = None

    if parent is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline:
        try:
            p = np.asarray(parent, dtype=np.float64)
            if p.ndim != 1:
                p = p.ravel()
            # Bound exact O(n^2) evaluation to a modest size.
            if 0 < p.size <= 20000 and np.isfinite(p).all():
                p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
                if float(np.sum(p)) >= 0.01:
                    p_score = _eval_list([float(x) for x in p])
                    # Prefer parent if it is strictly better, or essentially tied
                    # with the proven optimal unit vector.
                    if np.isfinite(p_score) and (p_score < best_score or abs(p_score - 2.0) <= 1e-12):
                        # If tied but parent is longer and exactly at the lower bound,
                        # using it is harmless.
                        best = p
                        best_score = p_score
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Final validation with the authoritative evaluator for modest best.
    try:
        b = np.asarray(best, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if b.size == 0 or not np.isfinite(b).all():
            return unit
        b = np.clip(b, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if float(np.sum(b)) < 0.01:
            return unit
        if b.size <= 20000:
            fs = _eval_list([float(x) for x in b])
            if np.isfinite(fs):
                # keep best; fs should equal best_score
                pass
        else:
            # If best unexpectedly became large, fall back to cheap unit.
            return unit
    except Exception:
        return unit

    out = [float(x) for x in np.asarray(best, dtype=np.float64).ravel()]
    if not out:
        return unit
    if not all(np.isfinite(float(x)) for x in out):
        return unit
    return out
```

Optimization subroutine:
```
def optimize(a0, time_limit, rng):
   n = len(a0); a = a0/s; L=fft length >=2n; maybe Lg >=3n.
   tau = initial_tau; steps...
   u = log(a + eps) maybe? Or use a directly with simplex projection.
```
Which optimization is most robust? Let's compare.

**Softmax Adam**:
- u initially log(a0). a=softmax(u). Softmax may underflow if u large; use shift.
- Gradient wrt u as above. Adam maintains moments. Step size maybe 0.01? Need tune.
- Homogeneity: LSE(c/τ) with c sum fixed? Softmax renormalizes a each step, so scale fixed sum1; no issue.
- Since objective scale-invariant, gradient along scale is not important.
- Adam can take many steps. But high-dimensional softmax gradient may be noisy.

**Projecting gradient on simplex**:
- a = a0.
- LSE grad g (scale ~? c ~1e-4, w ~1/2n, g ~2∑w a ~O(1/n)=2e-4. Step size maybe 10? If η=1, change ~0.0002, small. But can scale gradient by n to O(1). Score change small. Use line search? Or use normalized gradient direction d = g - mean(g?) and step in relative terms.
- Project to simplex: a = max(a - η g, 0); normalize. This is standard. Choose η adaptively.
- Since g can have positive and negative. It will shift mass from directions increasing active c to decreasing.
- Could use accelerated proximal gradient.

Maybe use **multiplicative weights (MW)**:
For a given LSE w, update a_j ← a_j * exp(-η * r_j) where r_j = g_j? Then renormalize. This keeps positivity and corresponds to softmax with u update. Equivalent to u ← u - η r_j where r_j = g_j/a_j? The natural gradient on simplex is `a ← exp(u - η (g/a - weighted mean))`. This can be large if a small. We can regularize with ε.

Let's derive natural gradient carefully. We want minimize F(u) with a=softmax(u). Let G=∂F/∂a (length n). grad_u = diag(a) G - a(a^T G). Let H_j = G_j/a_j. Then grad_u = a (H - s) where s=∑ a H = a^T G. Thus direction decreases u by η (H - s). Since multiplying u by constant changes a? softmax invariant to adding constant, but H-s orthogonal to a. Good. Update u ← u - η (H - s). This is equivalent to a_j ← a_j exp(-η(H_j - s)). This is multiplicative. H_j can be large if a_j small and G_j nonzero. But G is bilinear; if a_j small, H may blow. We can floor a. Alternatively use Euclidean gradient a update.

Maybe simplest: Use scipy `optimize.minimize` with L-BFGS-B on parameters u? It would require many gradient calls; n=4806, maybe okay? But L-BFGS black-box nonbounded; need bound sum? We can fix last parameter or project each iteration. Custom is better.

Let's test gradient computation formula. For a sum1 length n. c = linear convolution a,a. We can compute via FFT:
```
L = 1
while L < 2*n-1: L*=2
A = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
C = A * A  # not conjugate, because P^2, not |P|^2!
c = np.fft.irfft(C, L)[:2*n-1]
```
Important: use A*A, not A*conj(A). For real a, P(θ)^2 can have negative real frequencies? The DFT of linear convolution is P(ω)^2 (pointwise), yes. A from rfft, square (not abs). irfft returns real if C satisfies Hermitian symmetry. Since A[-k]=conj(A[k]), square: A[-k]^2 = conj(A[k])^2 = conj(A[k]^2), so okay. Use `A * A`. Good. This differs from autocorrelation; correct for np.convolve(a,a). Great.

Gradient g_j = 2∑_r w_{j+r} a_r (where w length 2n-1). We can compute correlation similarly using FFT with **convolution/correlation**. We can use full convolution? We need h_j = ∑_{r=0}^{n-1} w_{j+r} a_r for j=0..n-1. This is cross-correlation of w with a. We can compute via FFT of padded sequences:
Let Lg next pow2 >= (2n-1)+(n-1) = 3n-2. We want circular correlation: corr[t] = ∑_r w[(t+r) mod Lg] a_r. For t=0..n-1, with w zero-padded length Lg, a zero-padded, no wrap if t+r < Lg. Max t=n-1, r=n-1 =>2n-2 <3n-2, okay. `W = np.fft.rfft(w, Lg)`, `Ar = np.fft.rfft(a, Lg)`, corr = irfft(W * Ar? or W * conj(Ar)? Standard correlation: corr_full = ifft(fft(w) * conj(fft(a))) gives ∑ w[n+t] a[n] (with conjugate for complex). Let's verify: For real, ifft(fft(w)*conj(fft(a)))_m = ∑_n w_{n+m} a_n (circular) (positive lag). Yes. So `corr = irfft(W * np.conj(Ar), Lg)[:n]`. Then g = 2*corr. Test n=2: a=[x,y], c=[x^2,2xy,y^2]. If w=[1,0,0] (active c0). corr[t]=∑_r w[t+r] a_r. t=0: w0 a0=1*x; t=1: w1 a0 + w2 a1=0. g=[2x,0], correct derivative of c0 wrt a [2x,0]. If w=[0,1,0] (c1): t=0 w1 a0 = y? Wait w1*a0? a0=x, but derivative c1 wrt a0=2y? w_{0+r} for r=0 w0=0? Let's compute formula h_j=∑_r w_{j+r} a_r. For j=0: r=0 w0 x=0, r=1 w1 y? a1=y, so h0=y. For j=1: r=0 w1 x=x, r=1 w2 y=0 =>h1=x. g=[2y,2x], derivative of 2xy: [2y,2x]. Good. If using corr with W*conj(A): corr[0]=∑_r w_r a_r = w0*x+w1*y = y. corr[1]=∑_r w_{1+r} a_r = w1*x+w2*y=x. Good. So formula works.

Thus gradient cheap.

  compute c, score_surrogate

We can include a few candidates and score them with FFT surrogate (O(n log n)). For n=10000, very fast. Then exact verify best. This satisfies search and may improve.

Surrogate scorer:
```
def fast_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a,float); a[a<0]=0; s=a.sum()
    if s<0.01: return inf
    a = a/s
    L = 1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F*F, L)[:2*n-1]
    M = c.max()
    return 2*n*M
```
This matches continuous sum1; exact score scale invariant. FFT gives tiny imaginary discarded; c nonnegative small negative. Use max( c, 0). Good.

Generate candidates:
- linear: `np.arange(n)+1`
- for alpha in [0.75,1,1.25,1.5]? `np.arange(n)+alpha` normalized, but if alpha<0? positive ensure.
- reversed same.
- `np.linspace(1, n, n)` same.
- `np.linspace(1, 2, n)` equivalent scale.
- quadratic increasing but capped? `v=(arange+1)**2`; may score worse.
- cubic? Maybe not.
- `v = arange + 1 + beta*arange*(n-1-arange)/n^2`? concave/convex. Could create q flatter? We can try small beta values.
- two blocks: height 1 for first L and last L, zero middle, L=n//3.
But two blocks exact score maybe >linear for n large; surrogate will pick linear if better.

However, a candidate like `v = np.round?` no.

Let's design final code with candidate list and surrogate, but not too risky. Since we cannot test, need ensure no errors.

Pseudo:
```
import numpy as np
import time

def _fast_score(a, deadline=None):
    n = a.size
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    a = np.clip(a,0,1000)
    s = a.sum()
    if s < 0.01: return inf
    a = a/s
    L = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)[:2*n-1]
    # c can have tiny negative
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M < 0: M = 0.0
    return 2.0*n*M

def _ramp_alpha(n, alpha):
    return np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + alpha

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 5.0)
    # choose n
    default_n = 4806
    parent = None
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception:
        parent = None
    if parent is not None and parent.size > 0:
        if np.isfinite(parent).all():
            n = parent.size
        else:
            n = default_n
    else:
        n = default_n
    # cap and min
    if n < 100: n = default_n
    # choose maybe up to 12000 if parent smaller? If parent size between 100 and cap, keep.
    n = int(min(max(n, 1000), 12000))  # ensure decent length; if parent 4806, ok.
```
But if parent size 4806, n remains 4806. If parent size 100000, cap 12000. If parent invalid, default 4806; min max? maybe use default 4806. If we set min 1000, okay. But if parent size 500, we increase to1000 (score better). Good. If parent size 50000, cap12000. Using a different length than parent still allowed. But "use height_sequence_1 directly" maybe for parent candidate, we need if its size not equal n? We can evaluate parent of its original size separately and return it if better. Let's keep `parent_eval` if original size <=20000.

Candidate generation with selected n (maybe 12000): But if parent length 4806, we could use n=4806 to match exact parent and reduce eval. If parent length >12000, using 12000. If parent smaller (<1000), use 4806. Let's set:
```
parent_n = parent.size if parent valid else 0
if parent_n >0 and parent_n <= 20000: n = parent_n
else: n = 4806
if n < 1000: n = 4806
if n > 20000: n = 20000  # though parent_n ensured
```
This uses parent length if reasonable. For announced 4806, n=4806. If no parent, 4806. Good.

Candidate list:
```
cands = []
ar = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
for alpha in (1.0, 0.75, 1.25, 1.5, 0.5):
    a = ar + alpha
    if a[0] >0: cands.append(a)
# maybe normalized scaling not needed for surrogate, but for exact cap after scaling max1.
# quadratic
cands.append(ar + 1.0)  # same
# concave: ramp + beta * ar*(n-1-ar)/n
for beta in (0.2, -0.2):
    a = (ar+1.0) + beta * ar*(n-1-ar)/max(1,n)
    if np.all(a>0): cands.append(a)
# two blocks L=n//3
L=n//3
if L>1:
   a=np.zeros(n); a[:L]=1; a[-L:]=1; cands.append(a)

Let's draft code:
```
import numpy as np
import time

def _surrogate_score(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    if a.size == 0:
        return float('inf')
    a = a[np.isfinite(a)]
    if a.size == 0:
        return float('inf')
    a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
    if a.size == 0:
        return inf
    mx = float(a.max()) if a.size else 0.0
    if mx <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(mx):
        return inf
    a = a / mx  # scale max 1, score invariant
    s = float(a.sum())
    if s < 0.01:
        return inf
    a = a / s
    n = a.size
    L = 1 << (2*n - 1).bit_length()
    F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
    c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)[:2*n - 1]
    # remove tiny imaginary automatically by irfft real; clip negative
    M = float(np.max(c))
    if not np.isfinite(M) or M < 0.0:
        M = max(M,0.0)
    return float(2.0 * n * M)
```
If `a` after finite filtering changes length; okay. But if parent length 4806 with one nan, we filter, length changes; okay. For candidate linear, no nan.

```
def _to_list(a):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    a = a[np.isfinite(a)]
    if a.size ==0: return []
    a = np.clip(a,0.0,1000.0)
    mx = a.max()
    if mx <=0: return []
    a = a/mx
    if a.sum() <0.01:
       # scale up? Actually scaling down may make sum <0.01 if mx huge and few? mx<=1000, a/mx<=1, sum could be 0.001 if n=1 and value 0.001? But if original sum after clip maybe >0.01? If a has one 0.001, mx .001, a/mx=1, sum1. If all zeros skipped. So sum≥ #nonzero? after scaling max1, sum at least 1 if any positive? If values [0.001,0], scaled [1,0], sum1. Good.
    return [float(x) for x in a]
```

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 2.0)
    # parent
    parent = None
    try: parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    except Exception: parent = None
    parent_valid = False
    if parent is not None and parent.size>0:
       if np.isfinite(parent).all(): parent_valid=True
       else:
          p = np.where(np.isfinite(parent), parent, 0.0)
          if np.any(p>0): parent = p; parent_valid=True
    # n
    if parent_valid:
       n = parent.size
    else:
       n = 4806
    if n < 1000: n = 4806
    if n > 8000: n = 8000 if not parent_valid else min(n,8000) # if parent 9000, use 8000
    # if parent size >8000, we skip parent candidate but use n=8000.
    ar = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    cands = []
    for alpha in (1.0, 0.8, 1.25):
        a = ar + alpha
        if np.all(a>0): cands.append(a)
    # two blocks
    L = max(1, n//3)
    a = np.zeros(n); a[:L]=1.0; a[-L:]=1.0; cands.append(a)
    # maybe reverse ramp (same)
    # add parent if valid and size<=8000 (or maybe 10000 for surrogate)
    if parent_valid and parent.size <= 8000:
       p = np.clip(parent,0,1000)
       if p.size and p.max()>0:
           cands.append(p)
    # If no cands, linear
    best_arr = None; best_fast = inf
    for a in cands:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       sc = _surrogate_score(a)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_fast:
           best_fast = sc; best_arr = a
    if best_arr is None:
       best_arr = ar + 1.0
    # exact verify top if time
    # But if best_arr is a parent array, we need not scale? _to_list scales.
    # Maybe choose fallback linear always valid.
    fallback = _to_list(ar + 1.0)
    lst = _to_list(best_arr)
    if not lst: lst = fallback
    # exact verify if enough time and length not huge
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 2.0 and len(lst) <= 8000:
       sc = _exact_score(lst)
       if np.isfinite(sc):
           # If best failed or worse than fallback? We can compare with fallback exact? Avoid extra exact.
           # If sc is finite, keep.
           pass
       else:
           lst = fallback
           # maybe exact fallback if time?
    else:
       # If not verified, still return lst; but ensure finite.
       pass
    # Final safety: ensure list nonempty finite
    if (not lst) or any((not np.isfinite(float(x))) for x in lst):
       lst = fallback
       if not lst: lst=[1.0]
    return lst
```

Let's draft final code fully:
```
import numpy as np
import time

def _surrogate_score(a):
    try:
        a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if a.size == 0:
            return float('inf')
        a = a[np.isfinite(a)]
        if a.size == 0:
            return float('inf')
        a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
        mx = float(np.max(a)) if a.size else 0.0
        if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 0.0:
            return float('inf')
        a = a / mx
        s = float(np.sum(a))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
            return float('inf')
        a = a / s
        n = int(a.size)
        need = 2 * n - 1
        L = 1 << (need - 1).bit_length()  # if need is power? Let's use need.bit_length
```
Wait L choice: If need=7, bit_length=3 (8), L=8 >=7. If need=1, bit_length=1, L=2 >=1. Use `L = 1 << need.bit_length()`. Earlier. Good.
```
        F = np.fft.rfft(a, L)
        conv = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)[:need]
        M = float(np.max(conv))
        if not np.isfinite(M):
            return float('inf')
        if M < 0.0: M = 0.0
        return float(2.0 * n * M)
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')
```
Note: For `need=8191` bit_length13? 8191<8192 bit_length13, L=8192. Good. For need=8192 bit_length14 L=16384, still okay albeit larger; fine. Could use `L = 1 << (need - 1).bit_length()` for need power of two gives need, but not necessary. Keep simple.

`_make_list(a)`:
```
def _make_list(a):
    try:
       a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
       if a.size==0: return []
       a = a[np.isfinite(a)]
       if a.size==0: return []
       a = np.clip(a, 0.0, 1000.0)
       mx = float(np.max(a))
       if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <=0: return []
       a = a / mx
       # scaling to max1; ensure sum >=0.01 (it will, but check)
       if float(np.sum(a)) < 0.01:
           # amplify (will be clipped? if a max1, amplifying may exceed cap. But sum<0.01 impossible with max1 and size>=1)
           a = a * 100.0
       return [float(x) for x in a]
    except Exception:
       return []
```
Since after max scaling, at least one value=1, sum≥1, so no issue.

`_exact_score` as above.

`propose_candidate`:
```
def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    try:
       start = time.monotonic()
    except Exception:
       start = 0.0
    try:
       deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 2.0)
    except Exception:
       deadline = start + 1298.0 if start else 1300.0

    parent = None
    parent_valid = False
    try:
       parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
       if parent.size > 0:
           finite = np.isfinite(parent)
           if finite.all():
              parent_valid = True
           else:
              p = np.where(finite, parent, 0.0)
              if np.any(p > 0.0):
                  parent = p
                  parent_valid = True
    except Exception:
       parent = None

    if parent_valid:
       n = int(parent.size)
    else:
       n = 4806
    if n < 1000:
       n = 4806
    if n > 8000:
       n = 8000

    base = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    candidates = []
    for alpha in (0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0, 1.25, 1.5):
       a = base + float(alpha)
       if a.size and np.all(a >= 0.0) and np.any(a > 0.0):
           candidates.append(a)
    # two blocks
    L = max(1, n // 3)
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[:L] = 1.0
    a[-L:] = 1.0
    candidates.append(a)
    # maybe reciprocal ramp
    a = 1.0 / (base + 1.0)
    candidates.append(a)
    # square root ramp
    a = np.sqrt(base + 1.0)
    candidates.append(a)
    if parent_valid and parent.size <= 8000:
       p = np.clip(parent, 0.0, 1000.0)
       if p.size and np.max(p) > 0:
           candidates.append(p)

    best_arr = None
    best_score = float('inf')
    for a in candidates:
       if time.monotonic() > deadline:
          break
       sc = _surrogate_score(a)
       if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
          best_score = sc
          best_arr = a
    if best_arr is None:
       best_arr = base + 1.0

    candidate_list = _make_list(best_arr)
    fallback_list = _make_list(base + 1.0)
    if not candidate_list:
       candidate_list = fallback_list
    if not candidate_list:
       candidate_list = [1.0]

    # exact verification
    verified = float('inf')
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0 and len(candidate_list) <= 8000:
       verified = _exact_score(candidate_list)
       if not np.isfinite(verified):
           candidate_list = fallback_list
           verified = _exact_score(fallback_list) if time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0 else float('inf')
       else:
           # If a fallback candidate seems much better (possible surrogate/rounding), check it.
           if verified > 2.0 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
               fb_score = _exact_score(fallback_list)
               if np.isfinite(fb_score) and fb_score < verified:
                   candidate_list = fallback_list
                   verified = fb_score
    # ensure finite list
    try:
       if not candidate_list or any(not np.isfinite(float(x)) for x in candidate_list):
           candidate_list = fallback_list if fallback_list else [1.0]
    except Exception:
       candidate_list = [1.0]
    if not candidate_list:
       candidate_list = [1.0]
    return candidate_list
```

Full code:
```
import numpy as np
import time

def _surrogate_score(a):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size == 0:
            return float('inf')
        arr = arr[np.isfinite(arr)]
        if arr.size == 0:
            return float('inf')
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        mx = float(np.max(arr))
        if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 0.0:
            return float('inf')
        arr = arr / mx
        s = float(np.sum(arr))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
            return float('inf')
        arr = arr / s
        n = int(arr.size)
        need = 2 * n - 1
        L = 1 << need.bit_length()
        F = np.fft.rfft(arr, L)
        conv = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)[:need]
        M = float(np.max(conv))
        if not np.isfinite(M):
            return float('inf')
        if M < 0.0:
            M = 0.0
        return float(2.0 * n * M)
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')

def _make_list(a):
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size == 0:
            return []
        arr = arr[np.isfinite(arr)]
        if arr.size == 0:
            return []
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        mx = float(np.max(arr))
        if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 0.0:
            return []
        arr = arr / mx
        # With max 1 and at least one positive entry, sum is >= 1.
        if float(np.sum(arr)) < 0.01:
            return []
        return [float(x) for x in arr]
    except Exception:
        return []

def _exact_score(seq):
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except Exception:
        return float('inf')

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    try:
        start = time.monotonic()
    except Exception:
        start = 0.0
    try:
        deadline = start + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 2.0)
    except Exception:
        deadline = start + 1298.0

    parent = None
    parent_valid = False
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if parent.size > 0:
            finite = np.isfinite(parent)
            if bool(finite.all()) and bool(np.any(parent > 0.0)):
                parent_valid = True
            else:
                p = np.where(finite, parent, 0.0)
                if bool(np.any(p > 0.0)):
                    parent = p
                    parent_valid = True
    except Exception:
        parent = None
        parent_valid = False

    if parent_valid:
        n = int(parent.size)
    else:
        n = 4806
    if n < 1000:
        n = 4806
    if n > 8000:
        n = 8000

    base = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64)
    candidates = []
    for alpha in (0.0, 0.25, 0.5, 0.8, 1.0, 1.25, 1.5):
        a = base + float(alpha)
        if a.size and bool(np.all(a >= 0.0)) and bool(np.any(a > 0.0)):
            candidates.append(a)

    Lblk = max(1, n // 3)
    a = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    a[:Lblk] = 1.0
    a[-Lblk:] = 1.0
    candidates.append(a)

    a = 1.0 / (base + 1.0)
    candidates.append(a)

    a = np.sqrt(base + 1.0)
    candidates.append(a)

    if parent_valid and parent is not None and parent.size <= 8000:
        p = np.clip(parent, 0.0, 1000.0)
        if p.size and bool(np.max(p) > 0.0):
            candidates.append(p)

    best_arr = None
    best_score = float('inf')
    for a in candidates:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        sc = _surrogate_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
            best_score = sc
            best_arr = a

    if best_arr is None:
        best_arr = base + 1.0

    candidate_list = _make_list(best_arr)
    fallback_list = _make_list(base + 1.0)
    if not candidate_list:
        candidate_list = fallback_list
    if not candidate_list:
        candidate_list = [1.0]

    # Exact verification (authoritative evaluator) if time/resources allow.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0 and len(candidate_list) <= 8000:
        sc = _exact_score(candidate_list)
        if not np.isfinite(sc):
            candidate_list = fallback_list
            sc = _exact_score(fallback_list)
        else:
            # If the selected candidate is suspiciously far from the proven
            # good ramp, check the ramp fallback as well.
            if sc > 2.0 and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
                fb = _exact_score(fallback_list)
                if np.isfinite(fb) and fb < sc:
                    candidate_list = fallback_list
                    sc = fb

    try:
        if (not candidate_list) or any((not np.isfinite(float(x))) for x in candidate_list):
            candidate_list = fallback_list if fallback_list else [1.0]
    except Exception:
        candidate_list = [1.0]

    if not candidate_list:
        candidate_list = [1.0]
    return candidate_list
```

Algorithm `optimize(a0, time_limit, rng)`:
- n=len(a0). a = a0 normalized; if any <=0, floor small.
- Keep u = log(a + eps). To avoid underflow, update multiplicatively directly:
```
a = a0 / sum
eta = 0.1
tau = initial_tau (maybe M/2?)
best_local = a.copy(); best_local_score=score(a)
for t in range(iterations):
   c = conv_fft(a)
   M = max(c)
   score=2nM
   if score<best: update
   if M ==0 break
   # temperature
   tau = max(M/tau_factor_min, M0 * decay^t?)
   # weights
   z = (c - M) / tau  # <=0, stable; w=exp(z); if tau too small, normalize
   w = np.exp(z); w /= w.sum()
   # Add small noise for symmetry break? maybe rng.normal*tau
   corr = fft_corr(w,a)
   g = 2*corr
   # natural gradient direction H = g / a_safe; d = H - sum(a*H)
   a_safe = np.maximum(a, 1e-12)
   H = g / a_safe
   d = H - np.dot(a,H)
   # clip d
   d = np.clip(d, -D, D)
   # optional Adam moments
   u -= eta * d  (or a *= exp(-eta*d); normalize)
   a = a * np.exp(-eta * d)
   a /= a.sum()
   # enforce cap? values small, no.
```
But using u not necessary; updating a multiplicatively and normalizing accumulates. Over many iterations, some a may underflow to 0. We can periodically reset u = log(a+1e-20) if min too small? Or use `a = a * exp(-eta*d); a = np.maximum(a, 1e-20/n); a/=sum`.

Projection onto simplex:
```
def project_simplex(v):
    u = np.sort(v)[::-1]
    cssv = np.cumsum(u)
    rho = np.nonzero(u * np.arange(1, len(u)+1) - (cssv - 1) > 0)[0][-1]
    theta = (cssv[rho] - 1) / (rho + 1.0)
    return np.maximum(v - theta, 0.0)
```
This is standard.

Let's specify optimizer precisely.

```
def conv_coeffs(a):
    n=a.size; L=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    F=np.fft.rfft(a,L); c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,L)[:2*n-1]
    return c

def grad_from_w(w,a):
    n=a.size; L=1 << (3*n-1).bit_length()? For correlation length need (2n-1)+n-1=3n-2. Use Lg=1 << (3*n-1).bit_length().
    W=np.fft.rfft(w,Lg); A=np.fft.rfft(a,Lg)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(W*np.conj(A),Lg)[:n]
    return 2*corr
```
Can we reduce FFTs by reusing? Maybe not.

Surrogate score uses first FFT. Gradient needs second. For optimizer, we compute c then g. Good.

LSE weights:
```
z = c / tau
z -= z.max()
w = np.exp(z); w /= w.sum()
```

Use moments (Adam) on d to scale. d can have large components; Adam helps.
```
m = beta1*m + (1-beta1)*d

step = lr * m/bs / (np.sqrt(v/vs)+1e-8)
a *= exp(-step)
```

Softmax stable:
```
def softmax(u):
    u = np.asarray(u)
    umax = u.max()
    p = np.exp(u - umax)
    s = p.sum()
    if s == 0: p = np.ones_like(u)/u.size
    return p/s
```
For L-BFGS, u can have huge values; p underflow for many, but okay. If sum underflow (all exp zero except max? max exp=1 so sum≥1). Good.

def obj_grad(u, tau, scale):
   a=softmax(u)
   c=conv(a)
   M=c.max(); score=2n*M
   # LSE value

   # for L-BFGS, minimize maybe val - M/tau? Since M changes? We want LSE, but M/tau shifts depending on gradient of M? Optimization function:
```
def optimize_sequence(a0, deadline, max_tau_steps=5, max_iter=80):
    n=a0.size
    best=a0.copy(); best_sc=surrogate_score(a0)
    u=np.log(np.maximum(a0,1e-300))
    for tau_factor in [10,5,2,1,0.5,0.2]:
       M = best_sc/(2n) if finite else ...
       tau = max(M/tau_factor, 1e-12)
       # maybe choose tau initial = M/tau_factor
       state={'best_score':best_sc, 'best_u':u.copy()}
       def fun(u):
           nonlocal? use state
           a=softmax(u); c=conv(a); M=...; state update
           z=c/tau; handle finite; w=...
           g=grad_corr(w,a)
           s=np.dot(a,g); grad=a*(g-s)
           val = logsumexp(c/tau) * SCALE
           return val, grad*SCALE
       def cb(xk): return time.monotonic()>deadline
       res=minimize(fun,u,method='L-BFGS-B',callback=cb,options={'maxiter':max_iter,'maxfun':max_iter*2,'ftol':1e-12,'gtol':1e-6})
       u=res.x
       # update best from state
       if state.best_score < best_sc: ...
       if time enough and no improvement, maybe break
    return best
```

Functions:
```
import numpy as np, time, math
try: from scipy.optimize import minimize
except Exception: minimize=None

def _fft_conv(a, L=None):
    n=a.size
    if L is None: L=1 << (2*n-1).bit_length()
    F=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    c=np.fft.irfft(F*F,L)[:2*n-1]
    # due to rounding negative tiny; for score clip? For gradient, c should be true. Use np.maximum(c,0)? Convolution of nonnegative has nonnegative; set c[np.abs(c)<1e-12*cmax]=0; but clipping might alter gradient/lse. Use c = np.where(c<0 & >-eps,0,c).
    return c

def _corr_w_a(w,a,L=None):
    n=a.size
    if L is None: L=1 << (3*n - 1).bit_length()
    W=np.fft.rfft(w,L); A=np.fft.rfft(a,L)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(W*np.conj(A), L)[:n]
    return 2.0*corr
```
For score, using clipped c maybe: `c = np.maximum(c, 0.0)` before max; but if small negative due rounding, okay. For gradient LSE, clipping c before z? Should use actual c; small negatives negligible. Use `c = np.where(c < 0, 0.0, c)` in objective to avoid exp of negative? Negative lowers weight, okay. Clipping small negative to0 safe.

def _make_beta(n, alpha, beta):
   x=(np.arange(n)+0.5)/n
   a=x**(alpha-1)*(1-x)**(beta-1)
   a=np.maximum(a,0)
   s=a.sum(); if s>0: a/=s; else return None
   return a
```

def _random_mixture(rng, B, conc):
   w = rng.dirichlet(np.full(B.shape[1], conc))
   a = B @ w
   s=a.sum(); if s>0: a/=s
```

   zmax = float(np.max(z))

   if not finite or wsum <=0:

Now, softmax for L-BFGS: Implement helper:
```
def _softmax(u):
   umax=float(np.max(u))
   p=np.exp(u-umax)
   s=float(np.sum(p))
   if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0: return np.ones_like(u)/len(u)
   return p/s
```

Potential risk: L-BFGS may be slow for n=4806 if many evaluations; but budget large. Good.

Let's write final.
</think>

```python
import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize
except Exception:
    minimize = None


def _softmax_raw(u):
    u = np.asarray(u, dtype=np.float64)
    if u.size == 0:
        return np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(u)):
        return np.ones_like(u, dtype=np.float64) / u.size
    um = float(np.max(u))
    p = np.exp(u - um)
    s = float(np.sum(p))
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return np.ones_like(u, dtype=np.float64) / u.size
    return p / s


def _surrogate_score(a):
    """Fast FFT score for the exact normalized objective."""
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size == 0:
            return float("inf")
        arr = arr[np.isfinite(arr)]
        if arr.size == 0:
            return float("inf")
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        mx = float(np.max(arr))
        if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 0.0:
            return float("inf")
        arr = arr / mx
        s = float(np.sum(arr))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
            return float("inf")
        arr = arr / s
        n = int(arr.size)
        need = 2 * n - 1
        L = 1 << need.bit_length()
        F = np.fft.rfft(arr, L)
        c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, L)[:need]
        c = np.where(c < 0.0, 0.0, c)
        M = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
        if not np.isfinite(M):
            return float("inf")
        return float(2.0 * n * M)
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def _make_list(a):
    """Return a valid Python list scaling the candidate to maximum height 1."""
    try:
        arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if arr.size == 0:
            return []
        arr = arr[np.isfinite(arr)]
        if arr.size == 0:
            return []
        arr = np.clip(arr, 0.0, 1000.0)
        mx = float(np.max(arr))
        if not np.isfinite(mx) or mx <= 0.0:
            return []
        arr = arr / mx
        if float(np.sum(arr)) < 0.01:
            return []
        return [float(x) for x in arr]
    except Exception:
        return []


def _exact_score(seq):
    try:
        return float(evaluate_sequence(seq))
    except Exception:
        return float("inf")


def _make_beta(n, alpha, beta):
    try:
        x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
        a = np.power(x, alpha - 1.0) * np.power(1.0 - x, beta - 1.0)
        a = np.where(np.isfinite(a) & (a > 0.0), a, 0.0)
        s = float(np.sum(a))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
            return None
        return a / s
    except Exception:
        return None


def _optimize(a0, deadline):
    """Improve a normalized nonnegative sequence with L-BFGS + simple polish."""
    try:
        a0 = np.asarray(a0, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        a0 = np.where(np.isfinite(a0) & (a0 >= 0.0), a0, 0.0)
        s = float(np.sum(a0))
        if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
            return a0, float("inf")
        a0 = a0 / s
        n = int(a0.size)
        if n <= 0:
            return a0, float("inf")
        need = 2 * n - 1
        Lc = 1 << need.bit_length()
        Lg = 1 << (3 * n - 1).bit_length()
        scale = float(n) * float(n)
        stop_time = max(0.0, deadline - 8.0)

        best = a0.copy()
        best_sc = _surrogate_score(best)

        def conv_local(a):
            F = np.fft.rfft(a, Lc)
            c = np.fft.irfft(F * F, Lc)[:need]
            return np.where(c < 0.0, 0.0, c)

        def grad_local(w, a):
            W = np.fft.rfft(w, Lg)
            A = np.fft.rfft(a, Lg)
            corr = np.fft.irfft(W * np.conj(A), Lg)[:n]
            return 2.0 * corr

        def direct_descent(start, max_iter):
            nonlocal best, best_sc
            a = np.asarray(start, dtype=np.float64).copy()
            local_best = a.copy()
            local_best_sc = best_sc
            eta = 0.07
            floor = max(1e-12, 1e-9 / n)
            factors = [20.0, 10.0, 5.0, 2.0, 1.0, 0.5, 0.2, 0.1]
            per_factor = max(20, max_iter // max(1, len(factors)))

            for factor in factors:
                if time.monotonic() > stop_time:
                    break
                Mcur = local_best_sc / (2.0 * n) if (np.isfinite(local_best_sc) and local_best_sc > 0.0) else (1.0 / n)
                tau = max(float(Mcur) / factor, 1e-12)
                stalled = 0
                for _ in range(per_factor):
                    if time.monotonic() > stop_time:
                        break
                    c = conv_local(a)
                    Mval = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
                    sc = 2.0 * n * Mval
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < local_best_sc - 1e-13:
                        local_best_sc = sc
                        local_best = a.copy()
                        stalled = 0
                    else:
                        stalled += 1

                    z = c / tau
                    zmax = float(np.max(z)) if z.size else 0.0
                    w = np.exp(z - zmax)
                    ws = float(np.sum(w))
                    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
                        w = np.ones_like(c) / max(1, c.size)
                    else:
                        w = w / ws

                    g = grad_local(w, a)
                    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
                        g = np.zeros_like(a)
                    a_safe = np.maximum(a, floor)
                    H = g / a_safe
                    d = H - float(np.dot(a, H))
                    d = np.clip(d, -4.0, 4.0)
                    a = a * np.exp(-eta * d)
                    a = np.maximum(a, floor)
                    s2 = float(np.sum(a))
                    if not np.isfinite(s2) or s2 <= 0.0:
                        break
                    a = a / s2

                    if stalled > 80:
                        eta = max(0.005, eta * 0.75)
                        stalled = 0

            if local_best_sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = local_best_sc
                best = local_best.copy()
            return best, best_sc

        # L-BFGS on the log-softmax parameterization if available.
        if minimize is not None and time.monotonic() < stop_time:
            u = np.log(np.maximum(a0, 1e-300))
            factors = [20.0, 10.0, 5.0, 2.0, 1.0, 0.5, 0.2, 0.1]
            for factor in factors:
                if time.monotonic() > stop_time:
                    break
                Mcur = best_sc / (2.0 * n) if (np.isfinite(best_sc) and best_sc > 0.0) else (1.0 / n)
                tau = max(float(Mcur) / factor, 1e-12)
                info = [best_sc, u.copy()]

                def fun(x, tau=tau, info=info):
                    if x is None or x.size != n:
                        return 1e300, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    if not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
                        return 1e300, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    a = _softmax_raw(x)
                    c = conv_local(a)
                    Mval = float(np.max(c)) if c.size else 0.0
                    sc = 2.0 * n * Mval
                    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < info[0]:
                        info[0] = sc
                        info[1] = x.copy()

                    z = c / tau
                    zmax = float(np.max(z)) if z.size else 0.0
                    w = np.exp(z - zmax)
                    ws = float(np.sum(w))
                    if not np.isfinite(ws) or ws <= 0.0:
                        return 1e300, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    w = w / ws
                    g = grad_local(w, a)
                    if not np.all(np.isfinite(g)):
                        return 1e300, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    s_g = float(np.dot(a, g))
                    grad_u = a * (g - s_g)
                    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad_u)):
                        return 1e300, np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
                    val = zmax + float(np.log(ws))
                    return float(val * scale), grad_u * scale

                try:
                    def callback(_xk):
                        return time.monotonic() > stop_time

                    res = minimize(
                        fun,
                        info[1].copy(),
                        method="L-BFGS-B",
                        callback=callback,
                        options={
                            "maxiter": 90,
                            "maxfun": 320,
                            "maxls": 20,
                            "ftol": 1e-10,
                            "gtol": 1e-5,
                        },
                    )
                    # Record the final iterate as well, even if the callback already stopped near it.
                    if hasattr(res, "x") and res.x is not None:
                        ax = _softmax_raw(res.x)
                        cx = conv_local(ax)
                        Mx = float(np.max(cx)) if cx.size else 0.0
                        scx = 2.0 * n * Mx
                        if np.isfinite(scx) and scx < info[0]:
                            info[0] = scx
                            info[1] = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64).copy()
                except Exception:
                    pass

                if info[0] < best_sc:
                    best_sc = info[0]
                    best = _softmax_raw(info[1])
                    u = info[1].copy()
                if time.monotonic() > stop_time:
                    break

        # A short direct-descent polish is often useful near the lower bound.
        if time.monotonic() < stop_time:
            polish_iters = 1800 if (budget_s_global > 600) else 700
            best, best_sc = direct_descent(best, polish_iters)

        return best, best_sc
    except Exception:
        return a0, _surrogate_score(a0)


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1300, **kwargs):
    global budget_s_global
    budget_s_global = float(budget_s) if budget_s is not None else 1300.0

    try:
        start = time.monotonic()
    except Exception:
        start = 0.0
    try:
        deadline = start + max(0.0, budget_s_global - 12.0)
    except Exception:
        deadline = start + 1288.0

    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    # Use the supplied parent as one candidate and choose its length when reasonable.
    parent = None
    parent_valid = False
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if parent.size > 0:
            finite = np.isfinite(parent)
            if bool(finite.all()) and bool(np.any(parent > 0.0)):
                parent_valid = True
            else:
                p = np.where(finite, parent, 0.0)
                if bool(np.any(p > 0.0)):
                    parent = p
                    parent_valid = True
    except Exception:
        parent = None
        parent_valid = False

    if parent_valid and 1024 <= int(parent.size) <= 6000:
        n = int(parent.size)
    else:
        n = 4096
    n = int(min(max(n, 512), 6000))

    # Keep a small list of the best surrogate-scored candidates.
    top = []

    def consider(a):
        try:
            if a is None:
                return
            arr = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
            if arr.size != n:
                return
            arr = np.where(np.isfinite(arr) & (arr >= 0.0), arr, 0.0)
            if not bool(np.any(arr > 0.0)):
                return
            sc = _surrogate_score(arr)
            if not np.isfinite(sc):
                return
            arr = arr / float(np.sum(arr))
            if len(top) < 5:
                top.append((sc, arr.copy()))
                top.sort(key=lambda z: z[0])
            elif sc < top[-1][0]:
                top[-1] = (sc, arr.copy())
                top.sort(key=lambda z: z[0])
        except Exception:
            pass

    # Deterministic asymmetric candidates. Beta(1/2, 1) is a strong edge-singular start.
    beta_family = [
        (0.50, 1.00),
        (0.50, 1.10),
        (0.50, 1.25),
        (0.50, 1.50),
        (0.55, 1.00),
        (0.60, 1.00),
        (0.70, 1.00),
        (0.80, 1.00),
        (0.90, 1.00),
        (1.00, 1.00),
        (0.50, 0.80),
        (0.60, 0.70),
        (0.70, 0.60),
    ]
    for alpha, beta in beta_family:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        consider(_make_beta(n, alpha, beta))

    x = (np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(n)
    xe = np.maximum(x, 1e-9)
    ole = np.maximum(1.0 - x, 1e-9)

    # Explicit mixtures of an edge singularity with smooth backgrounds.
    for w in (0.85, 0.90, 0.93, 0.95, 0.97, 0.98, 0.99):
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        base = w * np.power(xe, -0.5)
        consider(base + (1.0 - w) * np.ones(n))
        consider(base + (1.0 - w) * (1.0 + x))
        consider(base + (1.0 - w) * (1.0 - x))
        consider(base + (1.0 - w) * (x * (1.0 - x)))

    # If the parent is available at the working length, include its clipped form.
    if parent_valid and parent is not None and parent.size == n:
        consider(np.clip(parent, 0.0, 1000.0))

    # Random nonnegative mixture search over a small basis.
    try:
        if time.monotonic() < deadline - 20.0:
            B = np.column_stack(
                (
                    np.power(xe, -0.5),
                    np.power(ole, -0.5),
                    np.ones(n),
                    x,
                    1.0 - x,
                    x * (1.0 - x),
                    x * x,
                    (1.0 - x) * (1.0 - x),
                )
            )
            max_random = 350 if budget_s_global > 300 else 80
            concentrations = [0.45, 0.7, 1.0, 1.6, 2.5]
            per_conc = max(8, max_random // max(1, len(concentrations)))
            for conc in concentrations:
                for _ in range(per_conc):
                    if time.monotonic() > deadline - 30.0:
                        break
                    wts = rng.dirichlet(np.full(B.shape[1], conc))
                    a = B.dot(wts)
                    consider(a)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Ensure at least one safe candidate exists.
    if not top:
        consider(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    if not top:
        final_vec = np.ones(max(1, n), dtype=np.float64)
        out = _make_list(final_vec)
        return out if out else [1.0]

    # Optimize the most promising candidates.
    best_arr = top[0][1].copy()
    best_sc = top[0][0]
    opt_deadline = max(start + 5.0, deadline - 25.0)

    for sc, arr in top[:3]:
        if time.monotonic() > opt_deadline:
            break
        try:
            opt_arr, opt_sc = _optimize(arr.copy(), opt_deadline)
            if np.isfinite(opt_sc) and opt_sc < best_sc:
                best_sc = opt_sc
                best_arr = np.asarray(opt_arr, dtype=np.float64).copy()
        except Exception:
            pass
        if time.monotonic() > opt_deadline:
            break

    # Final exact validation against the authoritative evaluator.
    candidate_list = _make_list(best_arr)
    fallback_list = _make_list(np.ones(n, dtype=np.float64))
    if not candidate_list:
        candidate_list = fallback_list
    if not fallback_list:
        fallback_list = [1.0]
    if not candidate_list:
        candidate_list = [1.0]

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 8.0 and len(candidate_list) <= 6000:
        sc_best = _exact_score(candidate_list)
        sc_fb = _exact_score(fallback_list)
        if not np.isfinite(sc_best):
            candidate_list = fallback_list
        elif np.isfinite(sc_fb) and sc_fb < sc_best:
            candidate_list = fallback_list

    try:
        if not candidate_list or any((not np.isfinite(float(v))) for v in candidate_list):
            candidate_list = fallback_list if fallback_list else [1.0]
    except Exception:
        candidate_list = [1.0]

    if not candidate_list:
        candidate_list = [1.0]

    return candidate_list
```
